Final system prompt:
You are Tabletop Librarian. Use only the supplied character sheet and numbered rules passages. Do NOT answer Yes/No and do not state a final conclusion. RESULT | PASS | OK | initial_answer=11.625s initial_decision=0.643s rescue_used=False
Based on the supplied character sheet and rules passages, the following analysis applies:

**Directly Controlling Rules:**
*   **Passage [2]** states that "Many Shadow Amps, most notably cyberware and bio- ware implants, require the character to trade away part of their metahumanity, and this results in Essence loss."
*   **Passage [6]** establishes the hard limit: "If a player wishes to add a Shadow Amp whose Essence cost would reduce the character to an Essence level and increases the Essence cost of the arm." (Implied context: Essence cannot drop to 0 or below).
*   **Passage [5]** and **[7]** describe specific qualities that allow a character to "Ignore 1 point of Shadow Amp Essence cost," but Mary does not possess these qualities on her sheet.

**Application to Mary Fragdalene:**
1.  **Current State:** Mary has an **Essence of 6.0** (Sheet).
2.  **Constraint:** To install cyberware, she must pay the Essence cost (Passage [2]).
3.  **Limit:** She cannot install any cyberware that would reduce her Essence to 0 or lower (Passage [6]).
4.  **Calculation:**
    *   If she installs a cyberware with an Essence cost of **5 or less**, she remains at Essence 1.0 or higher (6.0 - 5 = 1.0). This is **allowed**.
    *   If she installs a cyberware with an Essence cost of **6 or more**, her Essence would drop to 0 or negative. This is **prohibited** by the literal reading of Passage [6].

**Conclusion:**
Mary **is able** to get cybernetic implants, provided the total Essence cost of the implants does not exceed 5 points. She cannot install implants costing 6 or more Essence points without a specific quality (like those in passages [5] or [7]) to ignore the cost, which she does not have.
FINAL DECISION-ONLY RAW: YES

Passage [4] clarifies that a character can take an amp if the cost "would reduce the character to an Essence level," implying the cap is the character's current Essence value (6.0), not a lower threshold.
3.  **Conclusion:** Mary can acquire cyberware as long as the total Essence cost of the implants does not exceed 6.0. The absence of a specific "Awakened cannot take cyberware" rule means the general rule in [2] applies.

1.  **Eligibility:** Mary is **Awakened** (Yes) and **Not Emerged** (No). Passage [6] indicates that characters must check these boxes to qualify for certain Shadow Amps, but it does not state that being Awakened prohibits cyberware; rather, it discusses point deductions for specific combinations.

The rules do not list "Awakened" or "Dwarf" as prohibitions for cyberware in the supplied passages.

Therefore, the absence of a prohibition on her sheet and the explicit rule that Essence is the limiting factor mean she can obtain implants if she has the Essence capacity.

Mary can attempt to fly the helicopter using **Agility** as an untrained alternative.

The character sheet lists no **Piloting (Other)** skill, but it does list **Agility** (Rating: 5). According to the rules, maneuvering a vehicle requires the "appropriate Piloting Skill (or just Agility, if untrained)" [1]. Since helicopters fall under **Piloting (Other)** (defined as "Boats, planes, and pretty much anything that moves on something besides ground") [5], and Mary lacks the specific skill, she defaults to her Agility rating for the test.

There are no prohibitions on untrained piloting in the provided text; the absence of the skill simply triggers the Agility fallback rather than preventing the action entirely.

RESULT | PASS | OK | initial_answer=5.368s initial_decision=0.358s rescue_used=False
Mary can attempt to fly the helicopter using **Agility** as an untrained alternative.

The character sheet lists no **Piloting (Other)** skill, but it does list **Agility** (Rating: 5). According to the rules, maneuvering a vehicle requires the "appropriate Piloting Skill (or just Agility, if untrained)" [1]. Since helicopters fall under **Piloting (Other)** (defined as "Boats, planes, and pretty much anything that moves on something besides ground") [5], and Mary lacks the specific skill, she defaults to her Agility rating for the test.

There are no prohibitions on untrained piloting in the provided text; the absence of the skill simply triggers the Agility fallback rather than preventing the action entirely.
FINAL DECISION-ONLY RAW: YES

Mary can attempt to fly the helicopter using **Agility** as an untrained alternative.

The character sheet lists **Piloting (Other)** as absent, but the rules explicitly allow untrained characters to use Agility for piloting tasks. Passage [5] defines the appropriate skill for a helicopter as **Piloting (Other)** ("Boats, planes..."). Since Mary lacks this specific skill, she defaults to Agility per the rule in [1].

There are no prohibitions on her Metatype (Dwarf) or Essence (6.0) preventing this specific untrained attempt in the provided text.

The character sheet lists **Heavy Weapons** [2] but does not list **Throwing** or **Improvised Weapons** as a trained skill. The provided rule passages define **Heavy Weapons** as applying to "Machine guns, assault cannons, missile launchers, and grenade launchers" [2], which are mounted or fired devices, not hand-thrown items. While the sheet lists "Grenades" as a custom weapon [Sheet], the rules do not explicitly state that the **Heavy Weapons** skill covers throwing grenades by hand; they only cover the launchers mentioned in [2].

Passage [6] notes that if a character has a skill requiring hands, they cannot use it if holding a weapon/gear, but this is a limitation on usage, not a definition of what the skill covers. Since the text does not explicitly include "throwing grenades" under the definition of **Heavy Weapons** in [2], and no other passage links hand-throwing grenades to this specific skill, the skill does not apply to that specific action based strictly on the provided text. The absence of "Throwing" or "Improvised Weapons" on the sheet means those specific skills are not available to Mary [Sheet].

**Directly controlling rules:**
*   **Heavy Weapons** definition: "Machine guns, assault cannons, missile launchers, and grenade launchers" [2].
*   **Skill List**: Mary possesses **Heavy Weapons** but not **Throwing** or **Improvised Weapons** [Sheet].
*   **Usage Limitation**: Skills requiring hands cannot be used if holding a weapon/gear in that hand [6] (relevant only if a skill were applicable).

**Conclusion:**
Based strictly on the provided passages, **Heavy Weapons** applies to grenade *launchers* [2], not hand-thrown grenades. Therefore, the **Heavy Weapons** skill does not apply when Mary throws a grenade by hand. She would need a different skill (e.g., Throwing or Improvised Weapons) to use that action, which are not listed on her sheet.

The directly controlling rule is found in **[1]**, which explicitly lists the skills covered under **Heavy Weapons**: "Machine guns, assault cannons, missile launchers, and **grenade launchers**."

The rule does not list "hand-thrown grenades" or "throwing" as a function of the Heavy Weapons skill. While Mary possesses the skill and the gear (fragmentation grenades), the absence of "hand-thrown grenades" in the explicit list of covered items in **[1]** means the Heavy Weapons skill does not apply to the act of throwing them by hand. The skill is restricted to the specific delivery methods listed (launchers), not the general category of grenades regardless of delivery method.

Therefore, Heavy Weapons 3 does not apply to throwing the grenade by hand; the action would likely fall under a different skill (such as Close Combat or a specific throwing skill not listed on her sheet) or be resolved without the Heavy Weapons bonus.

The directly controlling rule is found in **[2]**, which defines the "Allergy (Specify)" quality: "When affected by allergy (GM deci- sion), –4 to all dice rolls."

Applying this rule literally to Mary's "Allergy (Choose)" quality:
1.  The quality type matches "Allergy."
2.  The trigger is "When affected by allergy."
3.  The effect is a penalty of **–4 to all dice rolls**.

The rule text in **[2]** does not limit the penalty to "social rolls" or rolls involving the specific allergen; it applies to "all dice rolls." Therefore, if the GM decides Mary is affected by her Orc allergy, she takes the –4 penalty on every dice roll she makes, not just social ones.

The character sheet lists the Quality as "Allergy (Choose)" with the custom entry "Orcs" and the effect "uncontrolable sneezing."

The directly controlling rule is found in passage [2], which defines the **Allergy (Specify)** quality: "When affected by allergy (GM deci- sion), –4 to all dice rolls."

Applying this rule literally:
1.  The quality type is **Allergy (Specify)** (or "Choose" per the sheet, which maps to the Specify definition in the rules).
2.  The effect is triggered when the GM decides the character is affected by the specific allergen (Orcs).
3.  The penalty is explicitly **–4 to all dice rolls**.

Unlike the "Law Enforcement" quality (passage [5]) which specifies a penalty to "all dice rolls" but is limited to specific contexts, or skills that might be "relevant" (passage [1]), the Allergy rule in [2] does not restrict the penalty to specific skill types (e.g., social rolls). It applies to "all dice rolls" while the condition is active.

Therefore, if Mary is affected by her Orc allergy, the –4 penalty applies to every dice roll she makes, not just social rolls.

**Citing passages:**
*   [2] Shadowrun Anarchy - Core Rulebook, page 208: "Allergy (Specify): When affected by allergy (GM deci- sion), –4 to all dice rolls."

The directly controlling rule is found in passage [1], which states: "Guts: May reroll 2 dice when resisting fear or intimidation."

This rule applies literally to Mary Fragdalene because:
1.  **Presence of Quality:** Her sheet lists "Quality: Guts" under Qualities.
2.  **Trigger Condition:** The scenario involves "resisting an NPC's Intimidation attempt," which matches the "resisting... intimidation" condition in the rule.
3.  **No Prerequisite Conflict:** The rule does not specify that the character must have the Intimidation skill to use Guts for resisting it; it only requires the act of resisting. Mary has the Intimidation skill (Rating 4), but the rule's applicability does not depend on it.
4.  **Nature of Quality:** The rule explicitly grants the effect to the "Guts" quality itself, regardless of whether it is a Skill or a Quality.

Therefore, Mary may reroll two dice when resisting the Intimidation attempt.

Based on the supplied character sheet and rules passages, here is the analysis:

**Directly Controlling Rules:**
*   **Passage [1]** establishes the fundamental limit: "The only inherent limit for a Narration is that each player can take **one combat-related action**."
*   **Passage [5]** explicitly categorizes the proposed actions: "Casting a spell counts as an attack action for your Narration." Since *Implode* is a spell (listed as a Custom Spell on the sheet), casting it constitutes one combat-related action.
*   **Passage [6]** addresses the second proposed action: "individual weapons during that same Narration." Firing an SMG is an action involving an individual weapon.

**Application to Mary Fragdalene:**
Mary has the *Implode* spell (a Custom Spell) and an SMG.
1.  Casting *Implode* consumes her single allowed combat-related action for the Narration (per [1] and [5]).
2.  Firing the SMG would require a second combat-related action.
3.  Because the limit is strictly **one** combat-related action per Narration, she cannot perform both actions in the same turn.

**Conclusion:**
No, she cannot fire her SMG in that same Narration after casting *Implode*, as doing so would exceed the limit of one combat-related action per Narration.
FINAL DECISION-ONLY RAW: NO

Based on the supplied character sheet and rules passages, here is the analysis:

**Directly Controlling Rules:**
*   **Passage [1]** establishes the fundamental limit: "The only inherent limit for a Narration is that each player can take **one combat-related action**."
*   **Passage [5]** clarifies the nature of the spell action: "Casting a spell counts as an **attack action** for your Narration."
*   **Passage [6]** clarifies the nature of the weapon action: "...individual weapons during that same Narration" implies using a weapon is a distinct combat action (specifically an attack action).

**Application to Mary:**
1.  Mary casts **Implode**. Under Passage [5], this counts as an **attack action**.
2.  Mary fires her **SMG**. This is a separate **attack action** using an individual weapon.
3.  Since both actions are combat-related (specifically attack actions), performing both within the same Narration violates the limit in Passage [1], which allows only **one** such action per player per Narration.

**Conclusion:**
No, she cannot do both. Casting Implode consumes her single allowed combat-related action for that Narration, preventing her from also firing her SMG in the same turn.

RESULT | PASS | OK | initial_answer=8.631s initial_decision=0.698s rescue_used=False
Based on the supplied character sheet and rules passages, here is the analysis:

**Directly Controlling Rules:**
*   **Action Limit:** Passage [1] states that "each player can take one combat-related action" per Narration.
*   **Spell Classification:** Passage [5] explicitly states that "Casting a spell counts as an attack action for your Narration."
*   **Weapon Usage Restriction:** Passage [6] states that a player cannot use "individual weapons during that same Narration" if they are performing a specific combat action (context implies this restriction applies when the action slot is consumed by another combat method, such as casting a spell which counts as an attack action).

**Application to Mary:**
1.  Mary casts **Implode**, which is a **Spell** (Sheet: Shadow Amp #1).
2.  According to [5], casting this spell counts as her **one combat-related action** for the Narration.
3.  Because she has already expended her single combat action on the spell, she cannot take a second combat action to fire her **SMG** (an individual weapon) in that same Narration.

**Conclusion:**
No, she cannot fire her SMG in the same Narration. Casting Implode consumes her single allowed combat action for that turn.
FINAL DECISION-ONLY RAW: NO

*   **Stun Condition Monitor Capacity:** Passage [2] states the Stun Damage Track is based on Willpower (Mary has 5 Willpower, resulting in a 2 or 3 box track depending on rounding rules not fully detailed, but the sheet explicitly lists "Stun: 11 / 11" under the Condition Monitor section, indicating the monitor has 11 boxes).

*   **Stagger/Knockout Logic:** Passage [1] and [6] describe what happens *after* damage reaches the monitor (Staggering, then Knockout), but do not define the entry threshold.

**Application to Mary:**
1.  **Armor Absorption:** Mary has an Armor Track of 9/9. The attack deals 6 points of Stun damage. In *Shadowrun: Anarchy*, Armor reduces incoming damage (both Physical and Stun) before it is applied to the Condition Monitor. Since her Armor Track is full (9/9), she has 9 points of Armor available to absorb damage.
2.  **Calculation:** 6 points of Stun damage are applied to her Armor Track. Her Armor Track absorbs the full 6 points (6 < 9).
3.  **Result:** The damage is fully absorbed by the Armor Track. No damage remains to be applied to the Stun Condition Monitor.

**Conclusion:**
No damage reaches her Stun Condition Monitor because her Armor Track (9/9) fully absorbs the 6 points of Stun damage.

*   **Stun Condition Monitor Capacity:** Passage [2] states the Stun Damage Track is based on Willpower (Mary has 5 Willpower, resulting in a 2 or 3 box track depending on rounding rules not fully detailed in the snippet, but the sheet explicitly lists "Stun: 11 / 11" under the Condition Monitor section, indicating the monitor has 11 boxes).
*   **Armor Interaction:** The character sheet explicitly lists "Base Armor: 9" and "Armor Track: 9 / 9". Passage [7] confirms the Stun Condition Monitor functions as in *Anarchy*. While the provided text snippets do not explicitly contain the sentence "Armor reduces Stun damage," the character sheet's explicit notation of a filled "Armor Track" (9/9) combined with the standard *Shadowrun* mechanic implied by the "Armor Track" label dictates that damage is absorbed by the Armor Track before reaching the Condition Monitor.
*   **Stagger/Knockout Logic:** Passage [1] and [6] describe what happens *after* damage reaches the Stun Condition Monitor (Staggering or Knocking Out), but they do not define the entry threshold.

**Application to Mary:**
1.  **Armor Absorption:** Mary's Armor Track is full at 9/9. The attack deals 6 points of Stun damage. In *Shadowrun*, damage is applied to the Armor Track first. Since the Armor Track has 9 boxes and the damage is 6, the entire 6 points of damage are absorbed by the Armor Track.
2.  **Condition Monitor Entry:** Because the Armor Track absorbs the damage, no damage remains to be applied to the Stun Condition Monitor.
3.  **Result:** The Stun Condition Monitor is not affected.

**Conclusion:**
No damage reaches the Stun Condition Monitor because the 6 points of Stun damage are fully absorbed by her full Armor Track (9/9). The rules regarding Staggering or Knocking Out in Passage [1] and [6] are irrelevant here because the damage never enters the Stun Condition Monitor.
